# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-06-04_TotalExtraction_79909053.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 10, 2024  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel (via 
Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff 
White (via Zoom).  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda).  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received 
by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson, item #16 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request 
by Dr. Aldrich, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Segrave, item #20(f) was 
removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve remaining items, seconded by 
Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 28, 2024.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for proposed budgets for the City of Cheyenne and 
Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2025.  There being no comments or input and purpose of 
the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for the Jolly Rogers 
Urban Renewal Plan and Project as required by Wyoming Statute § 15-9-109. Public comment 
was made by: Kathy Schaefer and Mark Christensen, AVI, agent for the applicant.  Comments 
were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins and Mr. 
Roybal.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, 
Mayor Collins closed the public hearing.  
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities. 
Reporting for Finance Committee, Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Cook voting “no”.  Comments were made by the following 
members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Segrave. (#4573)  
 
 

 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Chapter 5.78, Towing and Wrecker Recovery 
Operations, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, modifying provisions governing towing and wrecker recovery operation 
licensure and the Police Department’s towing and wrecker recovery operation rotation list. 
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated June 3, 2024, seconded 
by Mr. Johnson.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Main motion as amended by 
substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. 
Cook voting “no”.  Public comment was made by: Nick Winkler, Winkler Brothers Towing & 
Recovery.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich 
and Mr. Cook. (#4574) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2024 and ending June 30, 2025.  Reporting 
for Committee of the Whole, Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded 
by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  The following 
is a summary of the proposed budget: General Fund, $71,647,647; Weed and Pest, $737,334; 
Youth Alternatives Grants, $892,254; Recreation Programs, $2,450,333, Community 
Development Block Grant, $512,510; Law Enforcement Grants, $239,931; Miscellaneous Federal 
Grants, $1,090,364; Transportation Planning, $1,105,707; Transit, $5,905,504; Juvenile Justice, 
$168,887; Youth Alternatives Mentoring, $73,023; Development Impact Fees, $1,285,300; One 
Percent Sales Tax, $13,190,302; Specific Purpose Option Tax, $684,776; Youth Activities, 
$19,261; Golf Facilities, $528,500; Fleet Maintenance, $6,802,990; Surface Water Mitigation, 
$6,088,277; Solid Waste Management, $20,800,719; Civic Center, $2,251,464; Ice & Events 
Center, $972,375; Perpetual Care, $45,000; Total City, $147,637,616; Water Enterprise, 
$37,025,450; Sewer Enterprise, $22,653,420; Total Appropriation, $207,316,486. (#4575) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Defining the Terms and Conditions of employment for 
sworn officers of the Cheyenne Police Department for Fiscal Year 2024-2025, repealing and 
replacing Ordinance Nos. 4520 and 4539.  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Sections 1.4.3 Defined Terms and 2.2.6 
Temporary Use Approval of the Unified Development Code, adding a minimum of fourteen days.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. 
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.           
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 1.4.4.c Service Uses, Table 5-1 
Zoning District Uses, Section 5.7.4 Child Care Uses, and Table 6-2 Required Parking of the 
Unified Development Code, changing Child Care Requirements. Mr. Laybourn declared a conflict 
of interest and vacated council chambers. Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, 

seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present in council chambers. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating Section 10.24.190, Critical Traffic Control 
Areas; Creation and Administration; Enhanced Penalties, of the Municipal Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, authorizing the designation of Critical Traffic Control Areas and authorizing 
enhanced penalties as specified.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Public comment 
was made by: Connie Cruz, Sheryl Steel and Kathy Schaefer.  Comments were made by the 
following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, 
Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business to CB Community 
Business for land located northeast of the intersection of Cheyenne Street and Ridge Road – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. ROYBAL)   
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities to discharge 
uncollectible debts pursuant to Wyoming Statute 16-4-502.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#6411) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Supporting the adoption of a Vision Zero Policy to eliminate fatalities 
and serious injuries that are a result of crashes on city streets within the City of Cheyenne by 2040.  
Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Comments were made by the following 
members of the governing body: Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne. (#6412) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Approving the Jolly Rogers Urban Renewal Plan and the Jolly Rogers 
Urban Renewal Project pursuant to Wyoming Statute Section 15-9-110 – referred to Public 
Services Committee.  (SPONSOR – MR. ROYBAL)  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor to execute all necessary documents related to the 
transfer of a specified portion of the property located at 2014 Dell Range Boulevard – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. ROYBAL)  
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Expressing the appreciation of the Governing Body for the work of 
Director Nemecek, Director Sanchez, and their respective departments, on correcting deficiencies 
and improving and maintaining certain exterior components of the Municipal Building – referred 
to Public Services Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. SEGRAVE) 
 
[CA] 
Grant Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division, and the U.S. 
Department of Transportation for a Federal Transit Administration (FTA) Section 5307 Transit 
Capital Grant for the replacement of transit vehicles over the next three-year period (FTA Section 
5307 Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount of $1,528,788.00 with a match 
of $269,787.00). (#7984) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Metropolitan 
Planning Organization (MPO) and Quandel Consultants, Inc., for the Passenger Rail Station Site 

Selection Study (MPO Funds, County Funds and 2021 Specific Purpose Option Tax).  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $197,944.90). (#7985) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and The Design Studio, 
Inc. (TDSi), for the Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project (2021 Specific Purpose Option 
Tax Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $247,533.00). 
(#7986) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Just Be You, Inc. d/b/a 
Star Playgrounds, for the Saddle Ridge Playground Design and Installation Project (Development 
Impact Fees). Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $300,000.00). 
(#7987) 
 
 
Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Section 20 LLC for land 
located at the northeast corner of Powderhouse Road and East Carlson Street (Public Safety 
Infrastructure Fees).  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $836,325.00, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson. Dr. Aldrich moved to amend by substitute dated May 23, 2024, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried. Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body.  Public comment was made by: Mike White. Comment was made by the 
following member of the governing body: Mayor Collins.  (#7988)  
 
 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and William H. Smith and 
Associates (WHS), for design services for the Cheyenne - Three Alleyway Project (2021 Specific 
Purpose Option Tax).  Dr. Aldrich moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $110,246.00, 
seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor 
Collins, Mr. Cook and Mr. Laybourn. (#7989) 
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police 
Department, and Laramie County School District No. 1 for the School Resource Officers Program.  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7990) 
 
[CA] 
Industrial and Economic Development Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne 
and the Cheyenne/Laramie County Corporation for Economic Development (LEADS) for the 
period of July 1, 2024 to June 30, 2025 (General Fund) – referred to Finance Committee.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-06-04_TotalExtraction_76011252.pdf
 
Page 1 of 3 
 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE DEFINING THE TERMS AND CONDITIONS OF 
EMPLOYMENT FOR SWORN OFFICERS OF THE CHEYENNE POLICE 
DEPARTMENT FOR FISCAL YEAR 2024-2025, REPEALING AND 
REPLACING ORDINANCE NOS. 4520 AND 4539." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  Definitions. 
 
 
EMPLOYEE/EMPLOYEES - Employee or employees as used in this ordinance means 
sworn police officers, below the rank of chief. 
 
 
Section 2.  Applicability. 
 
 
The terms and conditions of employment as set forth herein shall apply to all employees as 
defined herein. 
 
 
Section 3.  Purpose. 
 
 
The purpose of this ordinance is to define the terms and conditions of employment for 
employees. The rights contained herein are in addition to any rights an employee may have under 
the Cheyenne Police Department Policy Manual (as that document may be amended at any time, 
before or after passage of this ordinance), and applicable federal, state and local laws. In areas of 
conflict between this ordinance and established federal, state or local laws, the latter shall govern.  
This ordinance comprises the exclusive statement of employee terms and conditions of 
employment. Any other agreement with or representation made to any employee by a supervisor, 
agent or officer of the City of Cheyenne that is in conflict with or in addition to this ordinance is 
specifically disclaimed by the City of Cheyenne, except for the Cheyenne Police Department 
Policy Manual and except for agreements entered into by and between supervisory personnel and 
any employee establishing specific job expectations, requirements or discipline, which agreements 
are part of the employee's official personnel file maintained by the Human Resources Department. 
 
 
Section 4.  Cheyenne Police Department Policy Manual and Benefits Policy. 
 
 
The Cheyenne Police Department Policy Manual including the benefit policy set forth in 
Exhibit A, as they exist or are amended from time to time, which is attached and incorporated 
herein by reference and made part of these terms and conditions of employment and shall apply 
only to sworn police officers. 
 
 
Section 5.  Compensation. 
 
 
The compensation paid to employees is set forth in Exhibit B, which is attached and 
incorporated herein by reference. The compensation appropriate for employees shall be computed 
9:19 am, May 21, 2024

 
Page 2 of 3 
 
with reference to the grade of the individual employee. Assignment of employees to the 
appropriate grade under the new plan will be made on a case-by-case basis by the chief with the 
assistance of the director of Human Resources, based upon employee hire date or promotion date. 
The governing body reserves the right, in its sole discretion, to modify or change pay scales. In 
addition to the compensation requirements imposed by this section: 
 
a) Effective July 1, 2017, employees will receive, in addition to basic pay, specialty on call 
pay as set forth in Exhibit C, attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference; 
 
b) Effective January 1, 2022, employees will receive, in addition to basic pay, longevity pay 
as set forth in Exhibit D, which is attached hereto and incorporated herein by reference; 
and  
 
c) Effective July 1, 2022, for any employee called to active military duty, the City of 
Cheyenne will compensate the difference between the employee's City base pay and total 
military monetary compensation (commonly listed on the military leave and earnings 
statement as the total entitlements), provided the military compensation is lower than the 
City base pay. Base pay is defined as the regular pay the employee would have received 
from the City had they not been called to active military duty not including on call or 
specialty pay. 
 
 
Section 6.  Continuation of Employment. 
 
 
Any employee covered by this ordinance who continues his or her term of employment 
during and beyond the term of this ordinance shall be entitled to advancement annually (subject to 
the provisions of Section 5), as set forth in Exhibit B, upon recommendation of the employee's 
supervisor, if the mayor and council approve funds to pay the increases. 
 
 
Section 7.  Health, Medical, Dental, and Life. 
 
 
The City will negotiate with or seek bids from qualified health insurance providers and will 
enter into a contract with a qualified carrier or carriers to provide employees with a medical 
insurance plan, which is on file in the Human Resources Department, for fiscal year 2024-2025.  
Each uniformed City employee eligible for health insurance coverage shall contribute ten percent 
(10%) of the monthly health insurance premium attributable to the level of coverage selected by 
that employee. This payment will be a payroll deduction. 
 
 
The City will maintain dental insurance on employees pursuant to the dental plan on file in 
the Human Resources Department. The City will pay premiums for employees. If an employee 
wishes to maintain dental insurance under this plan for eligible dependents as defined in Appendix 
B of the dental plan, he or she may do so by paying the premium for the additional person(s) 
insured. 
 
 
The City will pay premiums for term life insurance on all employees in accordance with 
the term life insurance plan on file in the Human Resources Department and City Clerk's Office. 
 

 
Page 3 of 3 
 
 
Section 8.  Term. 
 
 
The terms and conditions of employment set forth in this ordinance shall apply for fiscal 
year 2024 – 2025 and shall be approved as part of the budget process. This ordinance will terminate 
and be succeeded upon passage of a replacement ordinance. 
 
 
Section 9.  Repeal of Other Ordinances. 
 
 
All other ordinances establishing specific terms and conditions of employment are hereby 
replaced and repealed including Ordinance No. 4520 (August 31, 2023) and Ordinance No. 4539 
(January 13, 2024). 
 
 
Section 10.  Severability. 
 
 
If any clause, sentence, paragraph or part of this ordinance or application to any person or 
circumstance shall for any reason be adjudged by a court of competent jurisdiction to be invalid 
such judgment shall not affect, impair or invalidate the remainder of this agreement. 
 
 
Section 11.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its passage and 
publication.

Page 1 of 6 
 
EXHIBIT A 
 
EMPLOYEE WAGE AND BENEFIT POLICY 
 
THIS POLICY COVERS ALL COMMISSIONED OFFICERS OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
POLICE DEPARTMENT. 
 
1. 
MONTHLY WAGES. 
 
 
a. 
Wages. 
 
 
Effective July 1, 1997, all commissioned officers became covered by the City of Cheyenne 
pay plan. 
 
 
All step increases shall be on the officer's original date of employment or promotion date.   
 
 
b. 
Lateral Transfer Program. 
 
 
Lateral entry candidates must be currently certified as a Wyoming peace officer or in a 
state with a reciprocity agreement with Wyoming. If not currently certified in Wyoming, lateral 
entry candidates must take and pass the Wyoming POST challenge test, as well as the Wyoming 
criminal law and domestic violence courses offered through the Wyoming Law Enforcement 
Academy. 
 
 
Subject to these requirements, the department offers a lateral transfer program for officers 
with prior qualifying law enforcement experience. At the chief's discretion, qualified officers will 
be placed in the step program according to their experience as follows upon date of hire: 
 
 
 
2 to 4 years of experience: 
Grade 201 - Step 3 
 
 
4 to 6 years of experience: 
Grade 201 - Step 4 
 
 
6 + years of experience: 
Grade 201 - Step 5 
 
 
c. 
Specialty On Call Pay. 
 
 
Effective July 1, 2004, eligible employees will be entitled to receive specialty on call pay.  
Employees are limited to receiving compensation for a maximum of two (2) specialties per month. 
 
 
An eligible employee is one who serves as a first line supervisor (field training officer) or 
is required to be available for callout and does not receive any other compensation for being on 
call. 
 
 
d. 
Longevity Pay. 
 
 
Effective January 1, 2022, eligible employees will be entitled to receive longevity pay 
based on years of service. Longevity raises will be effective on the employee's anniversary month. 
 

Page 2 of 6 
 
2. 
OVERTIME. 
 
a. 
Patrol Officers and Patrol Sergeants. 
 
 
All sergeants and officers who are called in for overtime duties will receive a minimum of 
two and one-half (2.5) hours of overtime pay, excepting court appearances which are limited to 
two hours of overtime pay or compensatory time if the time spent is two (2) hours or less. All court 
time in excess of two (2) hours is to be paid at time and one-half the classification rate. All patrol 
officers and patrol sergeants shall be limited to eighty (80) hours of accumulated compensatory 
time, at which time, they must take paid overtime until they reduce below the eighty (80) hour 
level. 
 
 
All officers assigned acting sergeant duties will be compensated two (2) hours overtime 
for each shift. All sergeants assigned acting lieutenant duties will be compensated one-hour 
straight time for each shift. 
 
 
Should the acting sergeant, lieutenant or captain duties extend beyond thirty (30) 
consecutive days, the department may temporarily promote the officer, and place the officer in the 
appropriate pay grade of the new position. 
 
3. 
HOLIDAYS. 
 
 
The officers and sergeants of the City of Cheyenne Police Department will be entitled to 
those paid holidays that are set by Personnel Rules and Regulations of the City of Cheyenne, as 
amended from time to time, and as enjoyed by personnel of other departments of the City, to 
include "Special Holidays" or other paid time off afforded to other City employees. 
 
 
The rate of pay for these holidays will be computed on the basis of two thousand eighty 
(2,080) hours per year. The officer working a holiday has the option of taking overtime pay or 
compensatory time at one and one-half times the classification rate. If the holiday falls on an 
officer's regular day off, he will be given eight (8) hours of straight pay or compensatory time. 
Likewise, if the holiday falls on the officer's vacation, or while on sick leave, the holiday will not 
be counted as vacation time or sick leave. 
 
4. 
SICK LEAVE. 
 
 
Each officer shall accumulate sick leave time at the rate of ten (10) hours for each month 
of continuous employment to a maximum of six hundred forty (640) hours. 
 
 
Officers who have completed four (4) years of continuous service and terminate from the 
City shall receive fifty percent (50%) of their accrued sick leave at their regular base rate, not to 
exceed two hundred forty (240) hours. In the case of retirement, an employee will be reimbursed 
fully for his or her accrued sick time up to two hundred forty (240) hours. 
 
 
Officers who have not accumulated four hundred eighty (480) hours by January 20th will 
be entitled to twenty-four (24) hours of goodie time each year, provided they use not more than 

Page 3 of 6 
 
forty (40) hours of sick leave between January 21st of the prior year and January 20th of the current 
year. Officers who accumulate four hundred eighty (480) hours by January 20th will be entitled to 
forty (40) hours of goodie time each year, provided they use no more than twenty-four (24) hours 
of sick leave between January 21st of the prior year and January 20th of the current year; or thirty-
two (32) hours goodie time each year, provided they use no more than thirty-two (32) hours of 
sick leave between January 21st of the prior year and January 20th of the current year; or twenty-
four (24) hours goodie time each year, provided they use no more than forty (40) hours of sick 
leave between January 21st of the prior year and January 20th of the current year. Goodie days 
under this section shall not be accumulated. Officers entitled to the goodie time provided herein 
shall make application to the Chief of Police who shall schedule the goodie time having regard for 
the convenience of the department. Upon termination, the twenty-four (24), thirty-two (32) or 
forty (40) hours of goodie time are not paid as vacation. 
 
 
For a period of one (1) year following the date of an injury as defined in W.S. § 27-14-
102(a)(xi) sustained in the line of duty for the Cheyenne Police Department, an officer who is 
totally disabled from performing any duties for the Department shall receive a monthly payment 
from the City in an amount equal to the difference between: (1) the amount of any monthly benefit 
received by the officer from any division of the Wyoming Department of Employment; and (2) 
the average amount of monthly pay received by the officer during the twelve (12) month period 
preceding the date of injury, provided however, that if the officer has less then twelve (12) months 
of service prior to the injury, his or her average monthly pay shall be computed on the basis of 
actual months of service. Upon the expiration of a period of one (1) year following the date of an 
injury as defined in W.S. § 27-14-102(a)(xi), an officer who is totally disabled from performing 
any duties for the Cheyenne Police Department shall be required to use accumulated sick leave.  
 
5. 
SICK BANK. 
 
 
The Sick Bank is to be used by officers in the case of serious illness or injury which is not 
covered under Worker's Compensation, and after they have exhausted their accumulated sick 
leave. 
 
 
The Sick Bank is to be established on the effective date of this policy with a balance of one 
thousand two hundred (1,200) hours contributed by the City. The Sick Bank will maintain a 
balance limited to one thousand two hundred (1,200) hours. This balance to be renewed by the 
City, if any days are used, on January 1st of each year. 
 
 
A contributory pool (Sick Pool) of four thousand one hundred sixty (4,160) maximum sick 
hours will be established on the effective date of this policy. Sworn officers with excess sick 
leave—over six hundred forty (640) hours—may contribute their excess sick leave to the Sick Pool 
January 1st of each year. 
 
 
The Sick Bank and Sick Pool are to be administered by the Sick Bank Board. This Board 
will consist of the Chief of Police, the City Human Resources Director and the President of the 
Police Protective Association or his designee. 
 

Page 4 of 6 
 
 
Policies and procedures for administering the Sick Bank and Sick Pool are to be made by 
the Sick Bank Board, including renewing depleted balances. These policies and procedures are to 
be in writing and available, upon request, to all sworn officers. Request for Sick Bank/Sick Pool 
hours are to be made in writing to the Sick Bank Board, in accordance with their policies and 
procedures. All requests for Sick Bank/Sick Pool must be approved by the Board prior to use of 
those days. 
 
6. 
VACATION. 
 
 
Each officer shall be entitled to a vacation of eighty-eight (88) hours after one (1) year of 
continuous service, and shall be entitled to an additional eight (8) hours of vacation for each year 
of uninterrupted service provided that an officer is entitled to earn no more than two hundred 
sixteen (216) hours of vacation in any one (1) year. Insofar as possible, vacation time shall be 
determined by division and squad, with seniority rank having preference within each division and 
squad. 
 
 
Not more than two hundred ninety-six (296) hours of accumulated vacation leave can be 
carried from one (1) calendar year to the next calendar year. Accumulated vacation leave in excess 
of two hundred ninety-six (296) hours on January 1st of each year shall be forfeited.   
 
 
On separation from service, an officer shall be paid for all accumulated vacation hours 
earned and not taken, including the year of separation, proportionate to the time employed in that 
year. 
 
7. 
PERSONAL DAY. 
 
 
Each full-time uniformed police officer with one (1) year of continuous service will be 
awarded one (1) personal day each fiscal year. A personal day shall be defined as one (1) scheduled 
workday. 
 
 
This personal day may be used by the employee at his or her discretion, with supervisory 
approval. Personal days must be used in the year in which they are awarded. Personal days cannot 
be broken into increments of less than a normal working day. They do not carry over from one 
year to the next. 
 
8. 
REDUCTION IN FORCE. 
 
 
Officers laid off in accordance with the provisions of this section will be eligible for rehire 
or reinstatement into a position of the same classification in the inverse order of layoff. If rehired 
or reinstated, however, no officer shall accumulate time in service during the period of lay off. An 
officer laid off under these provisions shall be protected for a period of twenty-four (24) months 
from time of separation, pursuant to state statute. The department shall notify protected officers of 
any vacancy as soon as known to the department. The officer shall notify the department within 
five (5) days of his or her receipt of notice of his or her intent and must report at least fifteen (15) 
days after receipt of notice. 
 

Page 5 of 6 
 
9. 
CLOTHING AND EQUIPMENT ALLOWANCES. 
 
a. Detectives shall be entitled to eighty dollars ($80.00) per month clothing allowance. 
 
b. The department will provide all employees with the following equipment: 
 
 
 
i. 
Uniforms; 
 
 
 
ii. 
Ballistic vest; 
 
 
 
iii. 
Leather gear; 
 
 
 
iv. 
Handcuffs; 
 
 
 
v. 
Baton; 
 
 
 
vi. 
Duty t-shirts; 
 
 
 
vii. 
Handgun; and 
 
 
 
viii. 
Duty footwear. 
 
10. 
MEDICAL AND LIFE INSURANCE. 
 
 
All eligible commissioned officers may elect to participate in any of the health insurance 
plans offered by insurance companies contracted by the City to provide medical and life insurance. 
 
11. 
EXTENSION OF MEDICAL COVERAGE. 
 
 
a. 
All retirees may elect to participate in any of the health insurance plans offered by 
the City's health insurance provider. Premiums will be paid by retirees. 
 
 
b. 
If an officer sustains an injury as defined in W.S. § 27-14-102(a)(xi) in the line of 
duty for the Cheyenne Police Department and is awarded permanent total disability benefits as 
defined in W.S. § 27-14-102(a)(xvi), or if an officer dies as a result of an injury as defined in 
W.S. § 27-14-102(a)(xi) sustained in the line of duty, the officer and his or her eligible dependents 
shall be offered health insurance at the expense of the City subject to the following terms and 
conditions: 
 
i. 
The City's obligation to provide health insurance coverage for the officer 
shall terminate when the officer becomes eligible for coverage under Medicare or 
Medicaid; 
 
ii. 
The City's obligation to provide health insurance for the officer's spouse 
shall terminate when the spouse re-marries, becomes eligible for health insurance coverage 

Page 6 of 6 
 
under any employer-sponsored health insurance program, or becomes eligible for Medicare 
or Medicaid, whichever occurs first; and 
 
iii. 
The City's obligation to provide health insurance coverage for an eligible 
dependent of the officer shall terminate when that dependent reaches the age of 
twenty-six (26) years, or becomes eligible for Medicare or Medicaid, whichever first 
occurs. 
 
 
c. 
For the purposes of this paragraph (¶ 11), the word "spouse" shall refer only to the 
person to whom the officer is legally married at the time the officer is awarded permanent total 
disability benefits as defined by W.S. § 27-14-102(a)(xvi) and the phrase "eligible dependent" 
shall refer only to a child of the officer, including any child adopted by the officer before the child 
reached the age of fourteen (14) years. Stepchildren, foster children, and wards of the officer under 
any guardianship or conservatorship may be eligible dependents for coverage under the plan 
documents for health insurance coverage provided by the City.  
 
 
In the case of a qualifying event, the eligible person may change his or her election within 
thirty (30) days of said qualifying event or wait until open enrollment. 
 
 
The City of Cheyenne may from time to time require verification of permanent disability 
of member, of full-time student status or mental or physical disability. 
 
12. 
PPA BUSINESS. 
 
 
With prior immediate supervisor and division head approval, officers may conduct PPA 
business during working hours. 
 
13. 
DUES FOR MEMBERSHIP. 
 
 
The dues for membership in the PPA shall be set forth by the PPA by-laws. The City agrees 
to deduct this sum, as a payroll deduction, from such member's monthly paycheck and remit these 
dues to the PPA. 
 
14. 
NEW LANGUAGE. 
 
 
All proposed changes to the wage and benefit policy will be distributed in writing a 
minimum of thirty (30) days prior to the effective date. The staff officers of the department will 
be available to discuss and explain and take any comments relative to the proposed change. All 
other policies will be distributed in writing and once each year updated in this manual.

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTIONS 1.4.3 DEFINED TERMS AND 
2.2.6 TEMPORARY USE APPROVAL OF THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT 
CODE, ADDING A MINIMUM OF FOURTEEN DAYS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
to the Unified Development Code (UDC) that is the subject of this ordinance meets the review 
criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2.  That on May 20, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Section 1.4.3 Defined Terms, of Article 1, General Provisions, of the 
Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in 
this section: 
 
 
Temporary Use. A use or activity that is intended to occupy the site on a non-continuous 
basis for more than 14 days. 
 
Section 4.  That Section 2.2.6 Temporary Use Approval, of Article 2, Applications, 
Procedures and Criteria, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is 
hereby amended as set out in this section: 
 
2.2.6 Temporary Use Approval 
 
a. Intent. A temporary use approval is intended to provide flexibility for different uses 
within a zoning district, but due to the short duration, special conditions relative to 
the specific site, and other case-specific issues may be acceptable for a short and 
limited time. 
b. Applicability. An application for a temporary use may be initiated by the property 
owner or authorized agent for any use within an existing zoning district that 
occupies the site on a non-continuous basis for more than 14 days. 
 
Section 5.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 6.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTIONS 1.4.3 DEFINED TERMS AND 
2.2.6 TEMPORARY USE APPROVAL OF THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT 
CODE, ADDING A MINIMUM OF FOURTEEN DAYS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
to the Unified Development Code (UDC) that is the subject of this ordinance meets the review 
criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2.  That on May 20, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Section 1.4.3 Defined Terms, of Article 1, General Provisions, of the 
Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in 
this section: 
 
 
Temporary Use. A use or activity that is allowed in the district and which is intended to 
occupy the site on a non-continuous basis for more than 14 days. 
 
Section 4.  That Section 2.2.6 Temporary Use Approval, of Article 2, Applications, 
Procedures and Criteria, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is 
hereby amended as set out in this section: 
 
2.2.6 Temporary Use Approval 
 
a. Intent. A temporary use approval is intended to provide flexibility for different uses 
within a zoning district, and specifically allow the potential for uses that are not 
generally appropriate in a zoning district, but due to the short duration, special 
conditions relative to the specific site, and other case-specific issues may be 
acceptable for a short and limited time. 
b. Applicability. An application for a temporary use may be initiated by the property 
owner or authorized agent for any use within an existing zoning district that 
occupies the site on a non-continuous basis for more than 14 days.is not otherwise 
allowed. 
 
Section 5.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 6.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

PLTA-24-4 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
MAY 20, 2024 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code Section 1.4.3 and 
2.2.6. 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-24-4 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Sections 1.4.3 and 2.2.6 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the requests 
meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendments are to make clarifications to temporary uses. The amendment adds  that 
a use will be required to apply for temporary use approval if it occupies a location on a non-continuous 
basis for more than 14 days. Anything that is less than 14 days and not permanent will not require a 
temporary use application or approval.  
 
This text amendment is mainly to address temporary uses that happen around Cheyenne Frontier Days 
(CFD). Every year there are multiple temporary uses that tend to show up for just the week of CFD. We 
would then require them to apply for a temporary use application which has a review period of at least 7 
days. At the same time, many of the uses also need to get licensed with the Clerks office. There have been 
times where the temporary use application come in late and then there is a rush to get everything 
processed in time for the Clerks office to also issue the license. CFD is roughly a 10 day event, this 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-24-4 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
amendment would make it so that a temporary use application for any use proposed to operate for the 
duration of CFD would not be required.  
 
Staff chose 14 days because a Zoning Violations first notice gives 14 days to remedy the violation. The 
uses that come for CFD are here for just its duration so if they receive a violation letter they will have it 
remedied by the end of CFD as they generally leave once it concludes.   
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
Amend UDC Section 1.4.3, Defined Terms, specifically Temporary Use. 
 
Temporary Use. A use or activity that is allowed in the district and which is intended to occupy the site 
on a non-continuous basis for more than 14 days. 
 
Additionally, amend UDC Section 2.2.6, Temporary Use Approval. 
 
a. Intent. A temporary use approval is intended to provide flexibility for different uses within a zoning 
district, and specifically allow the potential for uses that are not generally appropriate in a zoning district, 
but due to the short duration, special conditions relative to the specific site, and other case-specific issues 
may be acceptable for a short and limited time. 
 
b. Applicability. An application for a temporary use may be initiated by the property owner or 
authorized agent for any use within an existing zoning district that occupies the site on a non-continuous 
basis for more than 14 days is not otherwise allowed. 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
This proposed text amendment is necessary to address an issue not anticipated in the 
Comprehensive Plan when the Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted. Temporary uses 
are meant to be short in duration and not permanent. Anything less than two weeks would not 
be considered permanent and is very temporary in nature.  
    
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
 
(b) The governing body may divide the city or town into districts 
of such number, shape and area as it deems necessary, and 
within those districts it may regulate and restrict the erection, 
construction, reconstruction, alteration, repair or use of 
buildings, structures or land. 
 

PLTA-24-4 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to change the 
initial duration of a temporary use to eliminate the need for temporary uses that are less than 14 
days in length. This is allowed by state statutes as the City can dictate the length of time and use 
of land.  
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment provides additional clarification for temporary uses and lines up temporary uses 
with zoning violation first notices. This amendment will also allow uses who participate in CFD 
have an easier time getting through City processes in time for the event.   
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Sections 1.4.3 and 
2.2.6 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the amendments meet the 
review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.

5.7.4 Child Care Uses 
In districts where child care uses are allowed, they shall meet the following standards in 
addition to all other lot and building standards for the district.  
 
a. Family Child Care Home (FCCH)  
1. Shall be located within the primary residence of the owner. 
2. No more than 10 children shall be permitted.

2. No more than 15 children shall be permitted.

c. Child Care Center (CCC) 
1. Shall not be located within a residence. 
2. All requirements of the Wyoming Department of Family Services shall be 
met. 
3. Parking will be required per Section 6.2.4, Table 6-2. 
 
d. Child Care provided as an accessory/secondary use to a commercial or industrial 
business is permitted in any zone district and must meet all requirements of the

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
MAY 20, 2024 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code Section 1.4.4.c, 5.1.4 
Table 5-1, 5.7.4 and 6.2.4 Table 6-2. 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-24-5 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Sections 1.4.4.c, 5.1.4 Table 5-1, 5.7.4, and 6.2.4 Table 6-2 as outlined in this staff report and 
recommended by staff, noting the amendments meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendments are to the Child Care requirements of the UDC. The proposed text 
amendments are to align UDC requirements with the Department of Family Services (DFS). The reason for 
the changes is due to the confusion as to what is required when someone wants to open up a new child 
care. The City’s regulations did not match what DFS required and made the process more difficult for new 
child cares.  
 
The first proposed changes are to the definitions. DFS has three types of Child Cares. Family Child Care 
Home, Family Child Care Center, and Child Care Center. Staffs proposed amendment is to match DFS with 
the definition of each. The UDC has four definitions for Child Cares which were similar to DFS but had 
different names and broke Child Care Centers into two, a minor and a major. Staff proposes changing 
Child Care, In-home-Minor to Family Child Care Home, change Child Care, In home-Major to Family Child 
Care Center, and change Child Care, Center – Minor and Major to just Child Care Center. DFS does not 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-24-5 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
delineate between a Child Care Center Minor or Major and just calls it a Child Care Center. These changes 
will make it less confusing as people read through DFS requirements and then look at the City zoning 
regulations as the names will be the same.  
 
The second recommended amendment is to allow Child Care Centers as Conditional Uses in the LR, MR, 
HR, NR-1, NR-2, and NR-3 zone districts. Staff’s reasoning is that the UDC allows Primary and Secondary 
schools as conditional uses within these zone districts and there only slight difference between a school 
and a child care. This would open up new opportunities for child cares in more locations while also having 
them go through a public process to determine if the location is suitable. Churches can often be found in 
residential zones, and a child care can be a weekday use of a church or a re-use opportunity of a church 
location should the church cease operation. 
 
The third recommended amendment is to 5.7.4, Child Care Uses. Staff proposes removing the table within 
this section and replacing it with standards that match DFS requirements. DFS has specific standards that 
all child cares are required to meet. Current UDC requirements add additional burdens to child cares that 
DFS does not require or allows alternatives to. For instance, the UDC requires an outdoor play area, where 
DFS allows for an increased indoor play area in place of not providing an outdoor play area. Staff feels that 
if an applicant is meeting all DFS requirements, the only child care specific zoning requirements we should 
really look at are the parking requirements (requirements standard to all uses in the zone district would 
still apply – such as applicable landscaping and pedestrian connections). The proposed amendments to 
Child Care Uses in 5.7.4 would also allow child care as a permitted accessory use to any permitted 
commercial or industrial use. This would allow a large-scale business to have a child care on-site for 
employees despite being in a zone which may not otherwise allow child care uses. 
 
The last recommended amendment is to add the parking requirements into UDC Section 6.2.4 Table 6-2. 
By placing the parking requirements into this section it would allow for additional ways to receive parking 
credits and flexibility. Staff proposes that Family Child Care Centers (in home) be required to have 1 space 
per employee and 1 visitor space. Staff proposes that Child Care Centers be required to have 1 space per 
employee, 1 space for each 20 children at maximum capacity, and 2 required spaces. As an example, if a 
Child Care Center has 60 children and 10 employees, the current UDC requirement would be 20 parking 
spaces. The ITE analysis would suggest only 14 parking spaces are needed. Staff’s proposed changes would 
require 15 parking spaces. A parking worksheet can be accessed here.   
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
Amend UDC Section 1.4.4.c, Service Uses, specifically Child Care In Home – Minor and Major, and Child 
Care Center Minor and Major. 
 
Child Care, In-home – Minor Family Child Care Home (FCCH). A Service use where preschool care and 
education is provided for 3 up to 10 children for a portion of the day in a residential dwelling within the 
owner’s primary residence. The children are not related to the immediate family except that the 
provider’s own pre-school children shall count towards the total. The owner’s infants, toddler and/or 
children ages three (3) to five (5) shall count toward the capacity. 
 
Child Care, In-home – Major Family Child Care Center (FCCC). A Service use where preschool care and 
education is provided for 11 up to 15 children for a portion of the day in a residential dwelling within the 
owner’s primary residence. The children are not related to the immediate family except that the 
provider’s own pre-school children shall count towards the total. The owner’s infants, toddler and/or 
children ages three (3) to five (5) shall count toward the capacity. 
 

PLTA-24-5 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
Child Care, Center – Minor. A Service use where preschool care and education is provided for under 30 
children on a regular basis in a non-residential building. 
 
Child Care, Center – Major. A Service use where preschool care and education is provided for 30 children 
or more on a regular basis in a non-residential building. 
 
Child Care Center (CCC). A Service use where preschool care and education is provided for children on a 
regular basis not located in any person’s residence. 
 
Amend UDC Section 5.1.4, Table 5-1, to match the definition changes and allow Child Care Centers as 
Conditional Uses in the LR, MR, HR, NR-1, NR-2, and NR-3 zone districts. See attachment 1. 
 
Amend UDC Section 5.7.4, Child Care Uses to the following: 
 
In districts where child care uses are allowed, they shall meet the following standards in addition to all 
other lot and building standards for the district.  
(Delete existing table and replace with the following below.) 
 
a. Family Child Care Home (FCCH)  
1. Shall be located within the primary residence of the owner. 
2. No more than 10 children shall be permitted. 
3. All requirements of the Wyoming Department of Family Services shall be met. 
 
b. Family Child Care Center (FCCC) 
1. Shall be located within the primary residence of the owner. 
2. No more than 15 children shall be permitted. 
3. All requirements of the Wyoming Department of Family Services shall be met. 
4. Parking will be required per Section 6.2.4, Table 6-2. 
 
c. Child Care Center (CCC) 
1. Shall not be located within a residence. 
2. All requirements of the Wyoming Department of Family Services shall be met. 
3. Parking will be required per Section 6.2.4, Table 6-2. 
 
d. Child Care provided as an accessory/secondary use to a commercial or industrial business is 
permitted in any zone district and must meet all requirements of the Wyoming Department of 
Family Services. 
 
Amend UDC Section 6.2.4 Table 6-2, to add Child Care Parking requirements. See attachment 2. 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 

PLTA-24-5 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
This proposed text amendment is necessary to address an issue not anticipated in the 
Comprehensive Plan when the Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted. DFS has changed 
its requirements over the years and the UDC has not been updated to match what changes have 
been made. Plan Cheyenne encourages changes that align with market demand and child cares 
are in demand, but the current regulations can make them challenging to fit into locations 
throughout the City. 
  
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
 
(d) All regulations shall be made: 
(i) In accordance with a comprehensive plan and designed to: 
(A) Lessen congestion in the streets; 
(B) Secure safety from fire, panic and other dangers; 
(C) Promote health and general welfare; 
(D) Provide adequate light and air; 
(E) Prevent the overcrowding of land; 
(F) Avoid undue concentration of population; and 
(G) Facilitate adequate provisions for transportation, 
water, sewerage, schools, parks and other public 
requirements. 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to align with 
DFS regulations and make things clearer for those looking to operate a child care.  
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment provides aligning regulations with DFS and reducing parking requirements 
slightly to help promote more child cares throughout the community. These changes will make it 
easier for those looking to start up a child care as it would match DFS requirements, making the 
process less confusing.    
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Sections 1.4.4.c, 5.1.4 
Table 5-1, 5.7.4, and 6.2.4 Table 6-2 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the 
amendments meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-06-04_TotalExtraction_26091995.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE CREATING SECTION 10.24.190, CRITICAL TRAFFIC 
CONTROL AREAS; CREATION AND ADMINISTRATION; ENHANCED 
PENALTIES, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, AUTHORIZING THE DESIGNATION OF 
CRITICAL 
TRAFFIC 
CONTROL 
AREAS 
AND 
AUTHORIZING 
ENHANCED PENALTIES AS SPECIFIED." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 10.24.190, Critical Traffic Control Areas; Creation and 
Administration; Enhanced Penalties, of Chapter 10.24, Rules of the Road Generally, of Title 10, 
Vehicles and Traffic, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby created to read: 
 
10.24.190 - Critical traffic control areas; creation and administration; enhanced penalties. 
 
A. There is created the critical traffic control program, which shall be empowered to designate 
one (1) or more critical traffic control areas in the city of Cheyenne as provided in this 
section. The program shall be administered by the program committee in accordance with 
this section, which committee shall be composed of the mayor or his or her designee, the 
city engineer or his or her designee, and the chief of police or his or her designee. The 
objective of the program shall be to identify areas in the city with unsafe or unlawful traffic 
speeds or other behaviors and to implement appropriate temporary remedial measures to 
address those behaviors. 
 
B. No critical traffic control area shall be designated under the program except as provided in 
this subsection and only after the receipt of a critical traffic control program complaint. 
The complaint shall be signed by not less than five (5) residents of the area that is the 
subject of the complaint, which residents shall represent different businesses or households 
affected by the alleged unsafe or unlawful traffic speeds or behaviors. Complaint forms 
shall be made available to the public by the city engineer. Any complaint submitted under 
this subsection shall specifically identify the unsafe or unlawful traffic speeds or behaviors 
that are of concern to the residents. 
 
C. The program committee shall review and evaluate the merits of any traffic control program 
complaint submitted in accordance with subsection (B) of this section. Should the 
committee determine the complaint has merit, the city engineer shall order a traffic study 
for the affected area, the results of which shall be submitted to the committee not less than 
fourteen (14) days after the date that the study was initiated. If the city engineer ordered a 
study to evaluate vehicle speeds, the study period shall include not less than two (2) 
weekdays and not less than one (1) weekend Saturday as data gathering points. In addition, 
a crash history for not less than the previous five (5) years shall be obtained and a crash 
data analysis shall be developed and summarized. If the results of the study substantiate 
the complaint, the program committee may designate the affected area as a critical traffic 
3:51 pm, May 22, 2024

Page 2 of 3 
 
control area. Nothing in this subsection shall be construed to prohibit the city engineer from 
commissioning a third-party professional engineer to complete the traffic study required 
under this subsection. 
 
D. Upon the designation of any critical traffic control area as provided by subsection (C) of 
this section, the program committee may implement appropriate temporary remedial 
measures to address the unlawful traffic speeds or other behaviors including: 
 
1. Establishing enhanced fines for speeding violations within the designated area, which 
fines shall be two (2) times the amount of the fine that would be imposed for the same 
violation in the same or similar area but for the traffic control area designation. If 
enhanced fines are implemented, proper signage shall also be installed to provide 
reasonable notice to drivers, which signage shall identify the beginning and end points 
of the critical traffic control area. An enhanced fine imposed under this paragraph is 
mandatory and may only be reduced by a municipal court judge after the court 
specifically finds that the defendant has no ability to pay the full fine amount and that 
no reasonable probability exists that the defendant will have an ability to pay the full 
fine amount; 
 
2. Installation of additional traffic control devices, including driver feedback radar signs, 
traffic control signals or traffic calming devices; 
 
3. Increased law enforcement presence for the purpose of strictly enforcing traffic laws, 
which increased presence may be incentivized through the authorization of overtime to 
be offered to police officers; and 
 
4. Any other appropriate temporary measures the committee determines may address the 
unsafe or unlawful traffic behaviors. 
 
E. Any critical traffic control area designated under this section shall be reevaluated by the 
program committee not more than fourteen (14) days after the implementation of any 
temporary remedial measures to determine if the measures are still appropriate under the 
circumstances. Should the committee determine the unsafe or unlawful traffic behaviors 
have been corrected, the critical traffic control area designation shall terminate and the 
measures implemented in accordance with this section shall be removed. Should the 
committee determine that the temporary remedial measures should be modified or 
continued, the measures shall again be reevaluated by the program committee not more 
than fourteen (14) days after the measures were modified or continued. Any remedial 
measure implemented under this section may be instituted on a permanent basis when 
necessary to maintain the safety of the previously designated critical traffic control area 
and if made permanent, shall not be subject to the reevaluation requirements imposed by 
this subsection. 
 
F. Nothing in this section shall be construed to limit or otherwise affect the authority granted 
under title 10, chapter 10.68 of the city code or the authority of the city traffic engineer 
under section 10.12.130 of the city code.  

Page 3 of 3 
 
 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 3.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS TO CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS 
FOR LAND LOCATED NORTHEAST OF THE INTERSECTION OF 
CHEYENNE STREET AND RIDGE ROAD." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
 
 
Section 2. That on June 3, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a 
public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this ordinance, 
and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Tracts 3 and 4, Sunny Side Addition, Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby changed to CB 
Community Business. 
 
 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-24-79: 3423 Ridge Road – Zoning Map Amendment (zone change)
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 4.07 acres of land from MUB Mixed-
use Business to CB Community Business. 
LOCATION:
East of and adjacent to Ridge Road between Charles St. and Cheyenne 
St.
APPLICANT: 
Western Equities, LLC
AGENT:
Casey Palma, Palma Land Planning
OWNER:
Zola Hedrick
ZONING:
Mixed-use Business
FUTURE LAND USE:
Mixed-use Commercial
PURPOSE: 
To allow for accommodate a commercial development masterplan for the 
parcel.
PREPARED BY:
Connor White, Planner II  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-24-79 
 
 
June 3, 2023 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Tracts 3 and 4, Sunny Side Addition to CB Community Business as 
shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
x 
UDC Article 5 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from MUB Mixed-use Business to CB Community 
Business for roughly 4.07 acres of land. The land is currently vacant. The applicant is proposing a commercial 
development with multiple uses.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. Plan Cheyenne also encourages infill development.  
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Mixed-use Commercial Emphasis. This 
category includes a wide variety of land uses and activities, focused primarily on commercial 
activities, but also including other employment, public, and residential uses. Although the 
 
Future Land Use Designation

Residential

MR 
Single-family Residential 

UDC-24-79 
 
 
June 3, 2023 
‘optimal’ zone for this designation is considered MUB, the future land use designation does 
support the CB zone as it is a zone that is primarily for commercial uses.  
 
This property is near to the intersection of College and Pershing, which is designated as a mixed-
use commercial activity center which encourages multi-neighborhood commercial uses. The 
types of businesses encouraged at these types of activity centers often include people traveling 
from a distance, and therefor the more automobile-oriented CB zoning is appropriate. 
 
Based on the policies and future land use map, CB zoning is appropriate at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
There is CB zoning to the south and the east of this property. The surrounding area consists of a 
mix of different residential and service uses. The CB zone would be compatible with the nearby 
properties as it is a commercial zone with similar use allowances, but with different design 
standards than the MUB zone. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The surrounding area better matches the expected development patterns of CB zoning. The 
MUB zone requires buildings to be set closer to the streets and have a certain amount of 
windows and other building design elements, whereas the CB zone pushes buildings further 
from the street and requires fewer building design elements. The CB zone is also meant for 
more vehicle-oriented areas which would be the case for Ridge Road.   
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
This is in an area with an existing street network. The CB zone generally anticipates larger blocks 
and is better suited to the likely development patterns in this situation.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB District is intended for Retail, Employment, Service and Civic uses that may serve a 
number of residential neighborhoods or other districts in meeting daily needs, discretionary 

UDC-24-79 
 
 
June 3, 2023 
needs, or other business functions. The district regulations are designed to encourage these 
uses, while also protecting the abutting and nearby areas.  
 
The CB District is applicable in areas where moderate- or large-scale business uses are 
appropriate, specifically larger Mixed-use Commercial Activity Centers, Community/Regional 
Activity Centers, or Community Business areas of the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
This property is in a Mixed-use Commercial Activity Center, adjacent to Ridge Road and is 
planned to be a multi-use parcel with more auto oriented businesses.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific proposal is for a potential car wash. However, if that project is not executed after 
approval, the CB zone offers a wide variety of potential auto-oriented uses at this site (including 
apartments).   
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No major concerns were raised by other revieing bodies. A general comment was provided but 
will be taken care of during development of the property. 
 
“Construction of Charles St. adjacent is expected for any further development applications.” 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, review comments were received expressing the appropriateness of the  
CB zone at this location. 

UDC-24-79 
 
 
June 3, 2023 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The CB zone is 
appropriate based on the future land use map, surrounding zoning, surrounding development patterns, 
and proposed development. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from MUB to CB. 
 
ATTACHMENTS:

2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map 
5. Differences in uses per zone
